### Sample #0
- **Question**: "What is the capital city of France?"
- **Correct Answer**: Paris
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine a map where the Seine river weaves through a city of lights, its historic boulevards forming a lattice that points directly to the heart of the nation."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Think of the city known as the 'City of Light', famed for its Eiffel Tower and romantic cafés."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "It’s the most visited city in the world, famous for the Louvre."

### Sample #1
- **Question**: "Which continent contains the Sahara Desert?"
- **Correct Answer**: Africa
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a vast golden sea of dunes stretching across the equatorial belt, its grains whispering stories of ancient caravans."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "The desert lies on the second‑largest landmass, home to the Nile."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "It’s the continent south of Europe."

### Sample #2
- **Question**: "What is the longest river in the world?"
- **Correct Answer**: Nile
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Picture a timeless waterway carving through deserts and savannas, its current a silver thread tying together millennia of civilization."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Think of the river that flows northward through Egypt."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "This famous African river is home to massive crocodiles, flows past the Great Pyramids, and is often associated with Cleopatra."

### Sample #3
- **Question**: "Which country has the highest population?"
- **Correct Answer**: China
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Visualize a sprawling tapestry of over a billion lives stitched together across plains, mountains, and river valleys."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It’s the East Asian nation with the Great Wall."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "This is the massive country where giant pandas live and people celebrate the Lunar New Year with red lanterns and dragons."

### Sample #4
- **Question**: "What ocean lies between Africa and Australia?"
- **Correct Answer**: Indian Ocean
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine a turquoise basin that swallows trade winds, connecting the Arabian Sea to the Southern Ocean."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It’s the ocean west of India."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "This warm, tropical body of water shares its name with a large South Asian country."

### Sample #5
- **Question**: "What is the largest desert on Earth?"
- **Correct Answer**: Antarctic Desert
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of a frozen wasteland where snow dunes stretch beyond the horizon, covering an entire continent."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It’s the desert covering Antarctica."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "This massive polar region is the coldest, windiest place on the planet, where almost no rain falls despite being covered in a thick sheet of ice."

### Sample #6
- **Question**: "Which mountain range separates Europe and Asia?"
- **Correct Answer**: Ural Mountains
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a spine of ancient rock cutting across the eastern European plain, a silent divider of continents."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Think of the range that runs north‑south through Russia."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "This long mountain chain is often called the 'stone belt' of Russia and shares its name with a major river and a region famous for its gemstones."

### Sample #7
- **Question**: "What is the capital of Japan?"
- **Correct Answer**: Tokyo
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Picture a neon‑lit metropolis where tradition meets technology, home to Shibuya’s crossing and ancient temples."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It’s the city that hosts the Imperial Palace."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "This massive city is the bright and bustling home of the 2020 Summer Olympics and the famous red and white tower that resembles the one in Paris."

### Sample #8
- **Question**: "Which river runs through Baghdad?"
- **Correct Answer**: Tigris
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine a winding waterway that has witnessed the rise and fall of empires along its banks."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It’s one of the two great rivers of Mesopotamia."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "This river shares its name with a large, striped orange and black jungle cat."

### Sample #9
- **Question**: "What country is known as the Land of the Rising Sun?"
- **Correct Answer**: Japan
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of an island nation where sunrise first touches the eastern shore, inspiring poetry and samurai legends."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It’s the archipelago east of the Korean Peninsula."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "This island nation is famous for its pink cherry blossoms, high-speed bullet trains, and a red circle on its national flag."

### Sample #0
- **Question**: "What does CPU stand for?"
- **Correct Answer**: Central Processing Unit
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the chip that orchestrates instruction execution, acting as the brain of a computer system."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It processes data and controls other components."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #1
- **Question**: "Which data structure uses FIFO ordering?"
- **Correct Answer**: Queue
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Picture a line where the first element added is the first one removed, like customers at a checkout."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It stands for First‑In‑First‑Out."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #2
- **Question**: "What programming language is known for its tagline 'write once, run anywhere'?"
- **Correct Answer**: Java
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine a language whose virtual machine abstracts away the underlying operating system."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It was created by Sun Microsystems in the mid‑90s."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #3
- **Question**: "What does HTML stand for?"
- **Correct Answer**: HyperText Markup Language
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the skeletal language that structures web pages with tags."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It defines headings, paragraphs, and links."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #4
- **Question**: "Which algorithm is used for sorting in O(n log n) average time and works by partitioning?"
- **Correct Answer**: Quicksort
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a divide‑and‑conquer technique that selects a pivot and recursively sorts sub‑arrays."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Its average case is n log n, worst case n²."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #5
- **Question**: "What does CSS stand for?"
- **Correct Answer**: Cascading Style Sheets
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the language that decorates HTML with colors, layouts, and animations."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It controls the visual presentation of web pages."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #6
- **Question**: "Which protocol is used to securely transfer files over the internet?"
- **Correct Answer**: SFTP
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Picture an extension of SSH that provides encrypted file transfer capabilities."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It stands for Secure File Transfer Protocol."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #7
- **Question**: "What is the primary purpose of a firewall?"
- **Correct Answer**: To control network traffic based on security rules
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine a barrier that inspects packets and decides which to allow or block."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It protects a network from unauthorized access."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "Control network traffic."

### Sample #8
- **Question**: "Which version control system uses commits, branches, and merges?"
- **Correct Answer**: Git
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of a distributed VCS created by Linus Torvalds."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It stores snapshots of a project over time."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #9
- **Question**: "What does SQL stand for?"
- **Correct Answer**: Structured Query Language
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a language designed for managing relational databases with declarative statements."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It lets you SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE data."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #0
- **Question**: "What artistic movement is characterized by bright colors and bold brushstrokes?"
- **Correct Answer**: Impressionism
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine a style that captures fleeting light, pioneered by Monet and Renoir."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It focuses on the impression of a moment rather than precise detail."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #1
- **Question**: "Who painted the Mona Lisa?"
- **Correct Answer**: Leonardo da Vinci
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the Renaissance polymath whose portrait holds an enigmatic smile."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "He also designed flying machines and studied anatomy."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #2
- **Question**: "What term describes a work of art created using only black and white?"
- **Correct Answer**: Monochrome
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a visual palette limited to a single hue and its shades."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It commonly refers to grayscale photography."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #3
- **Question**: "Which sculptor created 'The Thinker'?"
- **Correct Answer**: Auguste Rodin
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Picture a bronze figure deep in contemplation, emblematic of human introspection."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "He was a French artist of the late 19th century."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #4
- **Question**: "What is the primary material used in traditional Japanese ink wash painting?"
- **Correct Answer**: Sumi ink
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the deep black pigment derived from soot, applied with a brush on rice paper."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It is called 'sumi' in Japanese."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #5
- **Question**: "Which musical period follows the Baroque era?"
- **Correct Answer**: Classical
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine a shift toward clarity, balance, and structured form in the late 18th century."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Composers like Mozart and Haydn belong to this era."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #6
- **Question**: "Who composed the symphony known as the 'Eroica'?"
- **Correct Answer**: Ludwig van Beethoven
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the revolutionary work originally dedicated to Napoleon before being rededicated to heroism."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "He was a pivotal figure bridging the Classical and Romantic periods."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "Beethoven."

### Sample #7
- **Question**: "What is the term for a painting technique using tiny dots of color?"
- **Correct Answer**: Pointillism
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a method where optical mixing occurs as the eye blends individual pigments."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It was popularized by Georges Seurat."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #8
- **Question**: "Which architect designed the Guggenheim Museum in Bilbao?"
- **Correct Answer**: Frank Gehry
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the deconstructivist designer whose flowing forms redefined contemporary architecture."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "His work includes the iconic titanium-clad museum in Spain."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #9
- **Question**: "What does the term 'cinematography' refer to?"
- **Correct Answer**: The art of motion picture photography
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine the craft of lighting, framing, and camera movement that creates a film’s visual language."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It is the discipline behind a director of photography’s work."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "Motion picture photography."

### Sample #0
- **Question**: "What is the law that states supply creates its own demand?"
- **Correct Answer**: Say's Law
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a principle asserting that production inherently generates purchasing power in a market."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It is named after a 19th‑century economist."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #1
- **Question**: "What does GDP stand for?"
- **Correct Answer**: Gross Domestic Product
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the total monetary value of all finished goods and services produced within a nation's borders in a given period."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It measures economic output."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #2
- **Question**: "Who is known for the theory of comparative advantage?"
- **Correct Answer**: David Ricardo
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine a classical economist who argued that nations benefit by specializing in goods they produce most efficiently."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "His model explains trade benefits despite absolute disadvantages."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #3
- **Question**: "What is inflation?"
- **Correct Answer**: A sustained rise in general price levels
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision the erosion of purchasing power as each unit of currency buys fewer goods over time."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It is typically measured by the consumer price index."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "Rise in prices."

### Sample #4
- **Question**: "Which market structure is characterized by many sellers offering identical products?"
- **Correct Answer**: Perfect competition
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of a theoretical market where firms are price takers, and entry is unrestricted."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Supply and demand alone determine price."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #5
- **Question**: "What is a recession technically defined as?"
- **Correct Answer**: Two consecutive quarters of negative GDP growth
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a prolonged decline in economic activity measured by output, employment, and income."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It is marked by a drop in GDP for six months."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "Two quarters of negative GDP."

### Sample #6
- **Question**: "Who coined the term 'invisible hand' to describe market self‑regulation?"
- **Correct Answer**: Adam Smith
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the Scottish philosopher who argued that individual self‑interest unintentionally benefits society."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "He authored 'The Wealth of Nations'."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #7
- **Question**: "What does the term 'liquidity' refer to in finance?"
- **Correct Answer**: The ease with which an asset can be converted to cash without affecting its price
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine a market where assets are quickly bought or sold without slippage."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "High liquidity means low transaction cost and fast settlement."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "Ease of converting to cash."

### Sample #8
- **Question**: "What is the primary goal of monetary policy?"
- **Correct Answer**: To control inflation and stabilize the currency
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of central‑bank actions like adjusting interest rates to influence money supply."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It aims to maintain price stability and full employment."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "Control inflation."

### Sample #9
- **Question**: "Which fiscal tool involves government spending to stimulate the economy?"
- **Correct Answer**: Expansionary fiscal policy
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision increased public expenditure and tax cuts to boost aggregate demand."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It is used during recessionary periods."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #0
- **Question**: "Who wrote the treatise 'Critique of Pure Reason'?"
- **Correct Answer**: Immanuel Kant
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the German philosopher who explored the limits of human understanding and the conditions of experience."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "His work laid foundations for epistemology and metaphysics."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #1
- **Question**: "What is the philosophical concept that asserts reality is constructed by the mind?"
- **Correct Answer**: Idealism
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision the doctrine that ideas, rather than material objects, are the primary substance of reality."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It contrasts with materialism."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #2
- **Question**: "Who is known for the allegory of the cave?"
- **Correct Answer**: Plato
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine a dialogue where prisoners mistake shadows for reality, illustrating epistemic ignorance."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "He wrote this in 'The Republic.'"
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #3
- **Question**: "What term describes the ethical theory that actions are right if they maximize overall happiness?"
- **Correct Answer**: Utilitarianism
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the principle of 'the greatest good for the greatest number.'"
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Jeremy Bentham and John Stuart Mill are its key proponents."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #4
- **Question**: "Which philosopher coined the phrase 'I think, therefore I am'?"
- **Correct Answer**: René Descartes
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a rationalist who sought an indubitable foundation for knowledge through methodological doubt."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "His Latin formulation is 'Cogito, ergo sum.'"
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #5
- **Question**: "What is 'existentialism' primarily concerned with?"
- **Correct Answer**: Individual freedom, choice, and responsibility
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine a philosophy that emphasizes authentic existence in the face of an absurd universe."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Key thinkers include Sartre and Camus."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "Freedom and responsibility."

### Sample #6
- **Question**: "What does 'dualism' assert about mind and body?"
- **Correct Answer**: They are distinct, separate substances
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the view that mental phenomena cannot be reduced to physical processes."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Descartes championed this position."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "Mind and body are separate."

### Sample #7
- **Question**: "Which ethical framework evaluates actions based on duties and rules?"
- **Correct Answer**: Deontology
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a moral system where the rightness of an act is determined by adherence to universal maxims."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Immanuel Kant is a central figure."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #8
- **Question**: "What is the 'social contract' theory?"
- **Correct Answer**: The agreement by which individuals form societies and accept authority in exchange for protection of rights
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Picture a hypothetical pact where citizens consent to surrender some freedoms for collective security."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Philosophers Hobbes, Locke, and Rousseau elaborated on it."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "Agreement to form a society."

### Sample #9
- **Question**: "Who argued that 'existence precedes essence'?"
- **Correct Answer**: Jean‑Paul Sartre
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the French existentialist who claimed human beings first exist and then define themselves."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "His ideas emphasize freedom and responsibility."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #0
- **Question**: "Who is considered the father of psychoanalysis?"
- **Correct Answer**: Sigmund Freud
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine the Viennese neurologist who introduced concepts of the unconscious, id, ego, and superego."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "He developed the theory of dream interpretation."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #1
- **Question**: "What is the study of mental processes such as perception and memory called?"
- **Correct Answer**: Cognitive psychology
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the branch focusing on information processing, problem‑solving, and mental representation."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It contrasts with behaviorism."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #2
- **Question**: "Which disorder is characterized by persistent low mood and loss of interest?"
- **Correct Answer**: Depression
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a mood disorder where affective, cognitive, and somatic symptoms dominate daily functioning."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It is often treated with psychotherapy and medication."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #3
- **Question**: "Who developed the hierarchy of needs theory?"
- **Correct Answer**: Abraham Maslow
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the psychologist who proposed a pyramid of human motivation culminating in self‑actualization."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "His model includes physiological, safety, love/belonging, esteem needs."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #4
- **Question**: "What term describes a learned helplessness response to repeated failure?"
- **Correct Answer**: Learned helplessness
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine a state where an individual expects no control over outcomes after enduring uncontrollable stressors."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It was first demonstrated in animal experiments by Seligman."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #5
- **Question**: "Which brain structure is most associated with memory formation?"
- **Correct Answer**: Hippocampus
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of the medial temporal lobe region that consolidates short‑term memories into long‑term storage."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Damage to it causes anterograde amnesia."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #6
- **Question**: "What is the 'Big Five' personality model?"
- **Correct Answer**: Openness, Conscientiousness, Extraversion, Agreeableness, Neuroticism
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a five‑factor taxonomy that captures core dimensions of human temperament."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It is often abbreviated as OCEAN."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."

### Sample #7
- **Question**: "What does the term 'cognitive dissonance' refer to?"
- **Correct Answer**: The mental discomfort experienced when holding contradictory beliefs or attitudes
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Imagine the tension arising from conflicting cognitions that motivates attitude change."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "Festinger introduced this concept in social psychology."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "Mental discomfort from contradictory beliefs."

### Sample #8
- **Question**: "Which therapeutic approach emphasizes unconditional positive regard?"
- **Correct Answer**: Person‑centered therapy (Rogers)
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Think of Carl Rogers' humanistic method that fosters self‑actualization through empathy, genuineness, and acceptance."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It is a non‑directive, client‑focused counseling style."
- **Hint (Easy)**: "Person‑centered therapy."

### Sample #9
- **Question**: "What is the term for a false belief held despite contradictory evidence?"
- **Correct Answer**: Delusion
- **Hint (Hard)**: "Envision a firmly held conviction that persists even when reality disproves it, often seen in psychotic disorders."
- **Hint (Medium)**: "It is a core symptom of schizophrenia."
- **Hint (Easy)**: " the correct value."
